P6904:
P6905: **EX-30. The tick host's new-bar early return - EA 12274-12282.** New at V403. GLM's condition 3 requires the opening-tick verdict check to be inserted ahead of this return, because the closed-bar host returns before doing any work on a same-bar tick.
P6906:
P6907: ```mql5
P6908: 12274: void OnTick()
P6909: 12275:   {
P6910: 12276:    static datetime s_lastBarTime = 0;
P6911: 12277:    datetime currentBarTime = iTime(_Symbol, PERIOD_CURRENT, 1);
P6912: 12278:    if(currentBarTime == s_lastBarTime) return;
P6913: 12279:    s_lastBarTime = currentBarTime;
P6914: 12280:    //--- [P-UJIMPL-IMPL-1 v8 IE5] unconditional pool refresh in the new-bar path
P6915: 12281:    SrjUjPoolRefresh();
P6916: 12282:    LoadWorkingSet(1, currentBarTime);
P6917: ```
P6918:
P6919: Carried and presence-asserted: EA 349-378 (10.1), EA 10641-10656 (10.2), EA 10608-10634 (10.3), EA 11771-11785 (10.4), EA 11843-11850 (10.5), EA 11913-11938 (10.6), EA 12034-12069 (10.7), EA 7445-7457 (10.8), EA 7457-8069 (10.9), EA 8094-8429 (10.10), EA 8430-8476 (10.11), EA 2337-2378 (11.1), EA 11815-11825 (12.2). With EX-16 the byte divergence that v12 disclosed is closed.
P6920:
P6921: Literal three-dot sequences sit inside spliced regions at EA 7855, EA 10860, EA 10862, EA 11148 and EA 11151. They are EA comment and `Print` text, not builder elisions, and none occurs in any builder-authored sentence of this packet.
P6922: ### 15.3 The day-close leg
P6923:
P6924: **15.3.1 Marks, cap and initialiser.** Carried from v14 and unchanged: marks derive from the evaluated bar's own date with no pilot bound, the 32-entry cap no longer limits the verdict, and the lazy initialiser is displaced for this purpose because the test requires its completion flag. The day and week-close life booleans use a strict inequality against the exit bar time while the verdict uses an inclusive one, so that boolean cannot read true for its own day-close exit and stays zero under the repair (EA 11716 against EA 12017). Disposition: the life readers migrate to the repaired on-the-fly derivation, and the boolean is removed as evidence for day-close entirely rather than left reading zero.
P6925:
P6926: **15.3.2 The trigger is one pass late, and only the predicate is defective.** The closed-bar host evaluates the last closed bar (EA 12292) and returns before any work on a same-bar tick (EA 12278, EX-30). On the pass whose FORMING bar is the 23:55 bar, the evaluated bar time is the mark minus one period and the local at EA 11857 already holds the 23:55 bar's open. The single defective term is therefore the test at EA 12017, which fires when the mark is at or before the EVALUATED bar time - one pass later, at the next day's open. Required: fire and book only when the mark equals the forming bar's open, that is `barTime <= mark < barTime + PeriodSeconds`, with the verdict bar named as the forming bar, since only that naming is correct under this host. The fail-soft to the evaluated close at EA 11858 must fail visibly for the day-close leg rather than silently substituting.
P6927:
P6928: **15.3.3 Tick host and latch.** The opening-tick verdict check is inserted ahead of the new-bar early return at EA 12278, before the current-bar guard, so it runs on the first tick of the verdict bar. The once-per-instance verdict latch spans both hosts with no double fire, and a tick-level close request racing a broker stop or target resolves through the existing pending and retry rows rather than a parallel path.
P6929:
P6930: **15.3.4 The writer's firing tick.** The revision lag sentence contradicted itself and is removed: the writer fires at the close-boundary opening tick, on the same terms as day-close, and the first-out-bar pin moves so the revision fires at the first tick of the first out-of-session bar. The regression row labels move with it - the two baseline revisions are re-anchored to the out-of-session opening tick rather than to the bar after it, and the values themselves, 159.908 and 160.298, are unchanged and reproduce exactly with no tolerance. Target-passed is re-scoped to three cases: targets already passed at the open, modifications overtaken by a touch while failed or pending, and residual in-flight lag.
P6931:
P6932: ### 15.4 Q1 contract
P6933:
P6934: **15.4.1 Eligible sessions.** LONDON and NYAM only, with the close event at or after the fill. The admission-containment test at EA 1927 is scoped to the entry session's close, because as written it rejects every session run not containing the admission bar and would block every later-session revision. The pin's second revision source, the gapped POC, is in scope. The ASIA and PM exclusion stands as a council ruling with its reason, and is carried as a KNOWN GAP, not a settled rule: a journal census must show whether any ASIA or PM extreme would have been nearer than the chosen target on any trade, and his symmetric pin is untouched.
P6935:
P6936: **15.4.2 The writer, the mask, and the mode-specific registration.** One mechanism writes a revision: the sync helper's success path. The one-shot touch election writes no revision but does write its own latch fields, which migrate per instance. The swept mask is buffer-slot-indexed and does not apply to the own-bar walk; the buffer path keeps it unchanged. The managed snapshot at EA 10702 sets the active flag BEFORE the alert-only return and before the concurrency, lot, stops and no-trade aborts, and the reset has already destroyed a live record before the concurrency check - so registration moves after the confirmed fill, and the sequence and admission counters consumed before the fill have their disposition stated. In alert-only there is no fill, so the paper instance registers at the snapshot and its flag means "paper instance not yet model-closed", which is also the alert-only concurrency predicate. The modify helper carries the same mode guard as the close helper and prints a skip-no-send row.
P6937:
P6938: **15.4.3 Transition table, printed in full so the contract does not rest on history.**
P6939:
P6940: | # | State or event | Next | Binding notes |
P6941: | --- | --- | --- | --- |
P6942: | 1 | Fill in execute or tester | OPEN; identity registered after the confirmed fill; revision 0 equals the broker target, SYNCED | no instance on a failed entry |
P6943: | 2 | Eligible nearer closed-session target or gapped POC at or after the fill | revision n PENDING_SYNC; retarget row printed unconditionally; first attempt on the same pass | a new revision cancels the previous pending budget |
P6944: | 3 | Modify DONE, exact readback equal to the revision, SL unchanged | SYNCED(n) | one sync row per revision, never per bar |
P6945: | 4 | Retryable failure (TIMEOUT, CONNECTION, PRICE_CHANGED, TOO_MANY_REQUESTS, REQUOTE) | PENDING; at most three actual calls, one per M5 bar | market-closed is a visible deferral and spends no call |
P6946: | 5 | Permanent failure, unknown retcode, or the third failure | SYNC_FAILED(n); one EXIT-UNSYNCED row per revision | original SL and target intact; no market close |
P6947: | 6 | Target already passed at the open, inside stops or freeze, or overtaken by a touch while failed or pending | no call; revision unaccepted | no market close; no operator rule needed |
P6948: | 7 | NO_CHANGES | SKIP only when the readback equals the revision | a skip is not a call |
P6949: | 8 | Model touch while SYNCED | AWAIT_DEAL; MODEL_ONLY when no deal follows | the instance stays live |
P6950: | 9 | Model touch while PENDING or SYNC_FAILED | EXIT-UNSYNCED row and alert | masks no other leg |
P6951: | 10 | Identity absent with a full-volume exit deal | RESOLVED; retired | a target deal at the last-synced revision is valid; stale only after a newer revision confirms |
P6952: | 11 | BREAK | market close, three calls, one per eligible bar | success is DONE with a same-identity OUT deal |
P6953: | 12 | HTF | non-executing; stays live | HTF-off asserted at acceptance |
P6954: | 13 | DAY_CLOSE | as row 11 | price reference per 15.3.2; trigger per 15.3.3 |
P6955: | 14 | Close pending | CLOSE_PENDING; still managed | |
P6956: | 15 | Close terminal failure | CLOSE_FAILED, visible, still managed | other legs keep evaluating |
P6957: | 16 | Identity absent with a history lookup | resolve the deal; else orphan published and still awaiting broker resolution | history-lookup retries bounded |
P6958:
P6959: Same-bar precedence: SL, synchronized target, BREAK, HTF, DAY_CLOSE. An unsynchronized touch never outranks another winning leg.
P6960:
P6961: **15.4.4 Retcodes, references and posture.** No numeric retcode, ticket overload, history-selection call, close reason, hedging or netting posture, or asynchronous setting binds from memory; every one is verified against the current reference and the account before encoding.
P6962:
P6963: **15.4.5 Verdict flags.** `vBREAK` carries only a cumulative guard and loses solely in the priority chain (EA 11981, EA 12039-12043); `vHTF` is gated on no stop, target or break (EA 11992); `vDAY` on all four (EA 12013). The gates to migrate off the old target flag are named: EA 11992, EA 12013, EA 12034 and EA 12040.
P6964:
P6965: ### 15.5 Q3 contract
P6966:
P6967: **15.5.1 Per-term census of the confirmation rule, and the corrected rule.** This table is the fold's admission ticket. Every conjunct in the source and every clause in the pin resolves to a page term or an explicit disposition; a row resolving to neither blocks the fold. Source terms are at 11.1 and EX-1; pin text is spliced at 15.1.
P6968:
P6969: | # | Term | Where | Resolution |
P6970: | --- | --- | --- | --- |
P6971: | S1 | prior-candle arm | EA 2366-2367 | DISPOSED per form: RETAINED when the retest bar is B+1, REMOVED when it is B, because the confirmation-canonical pin makes the retest bar confirm if and only if it closes into setup-bias direction, so a bearish retest bar cannot bind in the same-bar form |
P6972: | S2 | close-side arm with its reclaim variant | EA 2368 | REMOVED in both forms: it judges the prior close |
P6973: | S3 | the close-side return | EA 2369 | REMOVED, same reason; it is also the baseline's measured failing term |
P6974: | S4 | doji test | EA 2370-2371 | RETAINED as a fail term, since a body-close-through requires a body |
P6975: | S5 | body direction and its return | EA 2372-2373 | RETAINED as the body arm |
P6976: | S6 | touch with the one-point band | EA 2374 | both bounds RETAINED as containment; the BAND REMOVED, zero margin |
P6977: | S7 | the touch return | EA 2375 | RETAINED as the touch arm's fail term |
P6978: | S8 | line read | EA 2351 | RETAINED at the evaluated bar, bound to the retest bar per form |
P6979: | S9 | reclaim parameter | EA 2338 | REMOVED with its arm; the single call site that passed it is named in the census below |
P6980: | S10 | exact-equality counters and their save/restore wrappers | EA 2361-2365 | REMOVED with the arm they measured; the wrappers are named as that arm's call-site dependency |
P6981: | P1 | touch OR body close through the line | pin 105, 112 | present: the two arms |
P6982: | P2 | zero margin, zero tolerance | pin 112 | present: band removed, equality stated |
P6983: | P3 | the prior close is not judged | pin 86 | present: S2 and S3 removed |
P6984: | P4 | judge the retest bar's OPEN side | pin 86 | ADDED - this was the v14 omission |
P6985: | P5 | the next close holding, no close back over to invalidate or break | pin 86 | present: the hold term |
P6986: | P6 | the retest bar confirms if it closes into setup-bias direction | pin 112 | present, and it is what removes S1 in the same-bar form |

P6988: | P8 | confirmation bar evaluated at the next pass; retest-bar polls never substitute | pin 114 | present: bar mapping unchanged |
P6989:
P6990: Let B be the confirmation bar and R the retest bar, R being B in the same-bar form and B+1 in the general form; the form is selected by an OR over both candidates, which is stated rather than assumed. Let L be the line read at the evaluated bar.
P6991:
P6992: CONFIRMED(B) if and only if:
P6993:
P6994: - OPEN SIDE (pin 86, added): R's open is on the setup side of the line, with inclusive equality - long: `open(R) >= L`; short: `open(R) <= L`. Inclusive because the registered case is an equality case: the retained telemetry gives a three-point body against a 14:35 close of 160.526, so the 14:35 open is 160.523, exactly the line, and a strict reading would refuse the settled trade. The convention matches the retest detector's own inclusive-with-tolerance boundary at EA 2232.
P6995: - TOUCH arm (pin 105, 112): R's range contains the line exactly, `high(R) >= L && low(R) <= L`, the band-free form of EA 2374; AND B closes into setup-bias direction; AND B's close holds on the setup side, `close(B) >= L` long and `close(B) <= L` short, equality holding.
P6996: - BREAK arm (pin 112): B's body closes strictly through the line in setup-bias direction; exact equality is NOT a break and lands on the touch and hold side. The body convention, crossing side and direction are pinned against the canonical text before encoding.
P6997:
P6998: **15.5.1a Diagnosis of the baseline failure, stated rather than implied.** On the retained telemetry the prior-candle arm PASSES for a long, the close-side arm FAILS by exactly one point, and the reclaim variant also fails. The June 11 miss is therefore precisely the term the prior-close pin removes, and that is the whole defect.
P6999:
P7000: **15.5.2 Caller, mirror and body census.** Thirteen predicate call sites are spliced at EX-10; the two contender probes are LIVE; the profile sites are TELEMETRY on the strength of the source comment at EA 8344; the inline mirror at EX-15 is migrated to the repaired terms or declared void - its equality column will otherwise diverge on every row; and the confirmation-poll body at EX-28 is classified as a possible third mirror, since it carries its own term fields. The reclaim call site and the equality counters are named with S9 and S10 above. All live, telemetry and mirror sites are diffed old against new across the full journal against the CORRECTED rule, with the band removal, the close-side removal and the per-form prior-candle disposition appearing as named classes, zero unexplained flips required, and every refused-to-confirmed flip mapped to a register row.
P7001:
P7002: **15.5.3 The branch, and the mechanism chosen.** Derived from the carried region at 10.11 and adopted as ruled: June 11 takes yield, the abort identity then mismatches on direction and anchor-bar time, and the abort is dropped. **Mechanism decision, made here because it is builder authority and no pin is in question:** for a FLIP-KILLED holder the matching deferred abort is consumed BEFORE the yield, and the challenger is then seeded fresh through the ordinary stages. Reason: it is the only candidate that satisfies the fresh-initialization acceptance rows the contract already requires - the regime classification, the alignment check and the mid-term-bias outcome must be printed for the challenger, and only a fresh seed produces them; it carries the stage-one and stage-two guards the challenger has not yet passed, which the acceptance forbids skipping; and it satisfies the flip-kill pin directly, because the new trade does not wait. The alternative prebind-shaped path is adopted for the NON-flip-killed yield family, where no fresh seed exists to run, subject to the binding conditions that it never read a stale carriage, that the once-only counters extend to it, and that it is a designed mechanism rather than an accident of the zeroed-zone behaviour now visible at EX-26 and EX-27. What this fold refuses to do: re-key the abort so it survives the yield, or invent a promotion path for the flip-killed case, or demote to a stage that would read the previous holder's seed carriage.
P7003:
P7004: **15.5.4 What the yield actually leaves behind, stated exactly.** The transfer re-keys the anchor line, direction, price and bar time, and zeroes the zone, the touch flags, the latches and the memo. It does NOT reset the state, the regime, the session, the divergence latch, the alert-armed flag, or the seed-leg and reseed carriers. Consequently a challenger that arrives by yield rather than by fresh seed inherits the previous holder's regime and stage-two status, which is why the flip-killed case is routed through the fresh seed instead. The stage-four zone re-read has a derived asymmetry - a long cannot rebind against a zeroed lower bound while a short always can - so the mechanism must not rely on that re-read in either direction; that behaviour is now on-page at EX-27 rather than inferred.
P7005:
P7006: **15.5.5 Return census, complete.** Carried dispositions stand. Added: the apply-abort site becomes the wrapper's consume under this contract, stated rather than implicit; the no-target, no-stop and lot-too-small returns sit in the released pipeline and are moot for a consumed abort but are listed; and the incumbent stop-reference return is labelled by the abort it follows.
P7007:
P7008: **15.5.6 Acceptance.** Registered identity 11 June, New York, USDJPY, LONG, Daily-POC. Selection ends at entry. Required rows: the open, high, low and close of both candidate bars with the line printed together, INCLUDING the open, because the body and open-side terms are otherwise unproven; the open-side, touch, hold and body terms each named on the pass that uses them; the failing term of the old predicate named on the flip; the fresh-initialization rows for the flip-killed route - regime, alignment, mid-term-bias - or, for the non-flip-killed route, the designed path's own named rows; the zone evidence; the stage-five gate rows including the divergence proof row; the admission, snapshot and signal rows with the entry reference exactly 160.524; the deal recorded separately with the structural offset as class one and any excess latency as class two, the latter measured against the FIRST TICK of the bar rather than the signal-time spread field; the session-use call; no 14:45-or-later selection evidence; and one accepted take scoped by pair, session, date, anchor and direction. Branch rows are stated per route with the counters once per bar, phase and identity. Ordering: the June 11 admission requires the June 5 New York position broker-closed by any Q1 closing leg; if it is open, the guard must block, and that block is correct behaviour. Preservation: 5 June New York silence on its observed kill, 9 June never-reseeded, 5 June London 09:45 at 159.948, 3 June 09:10, the seven EURUSD takes, one take per pair, session and day, cross-session independence. Register fields stay dropped: the historical "register row 26" citation is superseded by the measured fact that no such row exists and is struck.
P7009:
P7010: ### 15.6 Provenance and record hygiene
P7011:

No RECON57 physical row is cited in acceptance until the segment exists and is re-extracted, and the model-versus-broker divergence stays diagnosable only when a future run surfaces the close-attempt retcodes and deal-resolution rows. The June 11 chart values remain an operator-side gap that blocks nothing.
